Title: End-to-End Guarantees for Masked Diffusion Models under Conditional Mixing

  • Authors: Fareed Sheriff
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39444
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39444
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39444]] End-to-End Guarantees for Masked Diffusion Models under Conditional Mixing(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39444)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Masked diffusion models train a denoiser on masked cross-entropy and generate by unmasking many positions in parallel. Existing MDM sampling bounds assume an exact denoiser or leave its error uncontrolled while training bounds cover only the masking distribution on which the model was trained. We present an end-to-end guarantee for a learned denoiser under conditional mixing (CM): stationary sources (e.g. floored order-$r$ Markov and two-sided-Doeblin hidden-Markov sources) for which revealing distant visible tokens moves a masked token's posterior on average exponentially little in the distance with correlation length $\tau_c$. We show the empirical risk minimizer trained under any masking distribution attains per-token excess $\varepsilon'$ on that distribution from $\varepsilon'^{-\mathcal{O}(\tau_c\log|\mathcal{V}|)}$ training sequences, independent of sequence length $N$ -- one sample budget serves every decoding schedule when each schedule's denoiser is trained under its own weighting. We further show that the exponent's order $\Theta(\tau_c\log|\mathcal{V}|)$ is minimax-optimal up to constants; more efficient rates require added structure. With this, we bound the end-to-end KL as $\mathrm{KL}(\pi\Vert p_{\mathrm{alg}})\leq \varepsilon+N\varepsilon'$ under a dilated schedule of $T=\mathcal{O}(\tau_c\log(N|\mathcal{V}|/\varepsilon))$ denoising steps versus $\Omega(N)$ steps for a fixed random order on an explicit CM source. Finally, we show that block-autoregressive samplers chain across blocks with no added error and that a $\Lambda$-looped order-$r$ message-passing class on order-$r$ sources achieves polynomial sample complexity, with approximation error improving per loop until saturation at $\Lambda r\approx \tau_c\log(1/\varepsilon')$. Experiments on released and retrained models support our results' qualitative predictions.

Title: Smooth Action Chunking Via Movement-Primitive Representations for Imitation Learning

  • Authors: Shota Nomura, Takayuki Osa, Yasuhiro Kato, Kazuki Ota, Naoki Shitanda, Tatsuya Harada
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.RO
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39451
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39451
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39451]] Smooth Action Chunking Via Movement-Primitive Representations for Imitation Learning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39451)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Imitation learning has become a widely adopted approach for vision-based robot policies, owing to its conceptual simplicity, scalability, and ability to leverage large-scale datasets. As these policies have grown more capable, action chunking has become their standard interface. At each decision step the policy outputs several future actions at once. Backbones from diffusion policies to large vision-language-action models all share it. Whatever backbone produces the chunk, its temporal structure is learned only implicitly. The commanded trajectory is therefore jerky both within a chunk and between successive chunks. On real hardware this appears as visible vibration and as repeated saturation of the follower's velocity limit. We address this issue by incorporating a movement-primitive-based trajectory representation into action chunking. Specifically, a trajectory segment is represented as a linear combination of smooth basis functions, yielding smooth trajectories by construction while leaving the policy architecture, training objective, and inference procedure unchanged. The same representation also yields a closed-form, inference-time correction for the second source of jerk. We condition the predicted coefficients so that the new chunk passes through a via-point at the previous chunk's last commanded action. This correction needs no retraining and, unlike asynchronous Real-Time Chunking, applies to policies queried in one shot. We instantiate it on a one-shot (ACT) and an iterative (DiT) backbone and evaluate in simulation and on a real bimanual ALOHA platform. The representation lowers within-chunk jerk by an order of magnitude on the one-shot backbone and by 2.4 times on the iterative one, with task success statistically non-inferior. The conditioning then removes 73-84% of the boundary discontinuity, at negligible per-step cost.

  • Keywords: defense, robust
  • Abstract: Deep neural networks achieve strong performance across many tasks but remain vulnerable to adversarial perturbations. Adversarial training (AT) is one of the most effective defenses, yet it suffers from high memory cost on large models and the persistent clean-robust accuracy trade-off. Recent work introduces parameter-efficient fine-tuning, such as LoRA, into AT, but this constraint induces a notable robustness gap relative to full-parameter AT. In this work, we revisit parameter-efficient adversarial fine-tuning from a parameter space perspective. Title: Judging Before Propagation: Task-Graph Contextual Integrity for Multi-Agent Privacy

  • Authors: Hengyu Wu, Blaise Delattre, Yang Cao
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39532
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39532
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39532]] Judging Before Propagation: Task-Graph Contextual Integrity for Multi-Agent Privacy(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39532)
  • Keywords: privacy
  • Abstract: Multi-agent LLM systems increasingly rely on persistent memory and inter-agent communication. These capabilities create privacy risks that extend beyond individual interactions: a release appropriate in isolation may become inappropriate when combined with information previously disclosed to a recipient or propagated to downstream agents along the planned workflow. Existing Contextual Integrity (CI) mechanisms for agentic systems typically rely on local judgments that assess each disclosure in isolation, with or without regard to the recipient's history, but do not account for how information propagates downstream. However, compliance at the current boundary does not guarantee appropriateness across the workflow. We introduce Task-Graph Contextual Integrity (TG-CI), to our knowledge the first task-graph-native formalization of Contextual Integrity for multi-agent systems. TG-CI represents a workflow as a Multi-Agent Task Graph (MATG) and extends CI from atomic transfers to stateful, graph-level information flows by jointly considering an item's intended propagation scope and recipients' evolving disclosure states. We operationalize TG-CI through propagation-scoped authorization under two complementary policies: Full-Scope, which requires admissibility across the complete downstream scope, and Auto-Region, which confines each item to its maximal admissible region using lightweight frontier gates. We further introduce MAPLE-Bench, a benchmark of 144 cases across four scenarios that jointly vary MATG topology, disclosure history, compromised-agent placement, and item-level privacy policies. In our primary experimental configuration, TG-CI reduces the mean unauthorized disclosure count by 85.8-94.1% relative to the Local-History CI baseline, while retaining 93.6-96.1% of the baseline's task accuracy, with the two enforcement policies providing complementary privacy--cost--utility trade-offs.

Title: ROAD: Modern Adversarial Training for Simple One-Step Distillation at Scale

  • Authors: Xiaoou Li, Shan You, Tao Huang
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39539
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39539
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39539]] ROAD: Modern Adversarial Training for Simple One-Step Distillation at Scale(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39539)
  • Keywords: diffusion, transformer
  • Abstract: One-step distillation turns pretrained diffusion models into fast generators, but strong results often require substantial training machinery and compute. Pure adversarial distillation offers a simpler route, yet has struggled at modern text-to-image scale. To address this limitation, we propose ROAD (Representation-grounded One-step Adversarial Distillation), a one-stage framework that distills text-to-image models with one adversarial objective and a lightweight representation-space critic. Second, we observed a fixed random feature mixing leads to long-horizon degradation. Third, for our dense and vector-valued logits, we introduce Jacobian R1 which penalizes the full input-logit Jacobian, overcoming pooled R1's limitation of neglecting most of logit sensitivities. A single adversarial objective then suffices: in one step, ROAD reaches 11.03 FID on SDXL and 13.73 on SD3.5-M on COCO-10K, surpassing the multi-step teachers at a fraction of distribution matching's training compute.

  • Keywords: secure, protect
  • Abstract: Programmable garbled-circuit execution must balance the benefits of cross-program reuse against the cost of repeated work during secure execution. A complete garbled processor provides a fixed circuit for many programs, but its evaluator repeatedly visits processor-wide structures even when only a small part performs the current protected computation. We present RV2GC, which reconstructs the garbling boundary around a fixed library of RISC-V instruction circuits. Public scheduling and operand routing operate outside the circuit topologies; persistent label-encoded state connects independently invoked garbled instruction circuits and instruction-specific garbling-free paths through a common interface. A Conditional-execution Stack and protected commits support secret-dependent branches, bounded loops, and secret-indexed memory access under public scheduling, keeping instruction and memory-address sequences independent of private inputs. Across nine benchmarks, measured ARM2GC-to-RV2GC runtime ratios range from 12.81 to 4,043. In terms of circuit size, the largest listed instruction circuit has 3,386 gates, corresponding to 2.044% and 0.393% of the 256-byte and 4,096-byte Amber processors, respectively. Additional comparisons quantify schedule storage, communication, and the cost of secret-dependent execution.

Title: AB-Attention: Accurate Block Attention for Accelerating Video Diffusion Transformers

  • Authors: Bin Xu, Mo Zhang, Zhengfeiyang Zhang, Xinfeng Yang, Yixing Ma, Wei Wang, HeXin Wang, Pengfei Zhou, Wangbo Zhao
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39663
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39663
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39663]] AB-Attention: Accurate Block Attention for Accelerating Video Diffusion Transformers(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39663)
  • Keywords: diffusion, transformer
  • Abstract: Video diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos, but the quadratic cost of 3D full attention dominates their inference. Dynamic block-sparse attention reduces this cost by computing exact attention only on key/value blocks selected from current activations. However, its fidelity degrades under aggressive sparsity: blocks are scored from coarse summaries or formed by costly global clustering, pruned blocks vanish from the softmax, and whole tiles are kept or dropped even when only a few of their keys matter. To address these limitations, we propose Accurate Block Attention (AB-Attention), a training-free method that accurately approximates dense attention at high sparsity. Its Intra-Block Clustering Selector (IBCS) estimates block importance faithfully yet cheaply by clustering queries only within each query block, without permuting tokens. Sampling with Inclusion-Probability Weighting (SIP) then samples blocks in proportion to these estimates and reweights them by inverse inclusion probability, so that one pass estimates dense attention rather than truncating it. Finally, its kernel gathers key/value blocks much smaller than a tile into full tensor-core tiles, refining selection at no extra tensor-core cost. On Wan2.1 and HunyuanVideo, AB-Attention skips 89--92\% of attention, whereas the baselines compute 1.8--3.6$\times$ as much, yet it still improves PSNR by up to 4.4~dB over the strongest baseline and achieves up to 2.23$\times$ end-to-end speedup. Our code is available at this https URL.

Title: Event-SAMA: Language-Grounded Video Object Segmentation on Neuromorphic Event Streams

  • Authors: Sergei Fedorchenko, Max Zehnder, Nikola Zubić, Rong Zou, Davide Scaramuzza
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39676
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39676
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39676]] Event-SAMA: Language-Grounded Video Object Segmentation on Neuromorphic Event Streams(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39676)
  • Keywords: segmentation
  • Abstract: Event cameras offer microsecond temporal resolution, high dynamic range and no motion blur, but referring video object segmentation (RVOS) lacks event-stream datasets and models. We introduce Event-SAMA-239K, a synthetic-event conversion of the SAMA-239K grounded video-chat corpus and all additional SAMA training data: 30,650 video clips and 392,162 images. Optical-flow, FILM or saccade upsampling followed by a linear event generator preserves all mask, referring-expression and dialogue annotations. We also introduce Event-SAMA, adapting SAMA-4B with a two-channel polarity patch embedding initialised from pretrained RGB weights and a zero-initialised event adapter. End-to-end training yields 24.37 $\mathcal{J}\&\mathcal{F}$ on MeViS valid_u and 22.33 $\mathcal{J}\&\mathcal{F}$ on the converted ReVOS valid subset, establishing the first event-native RVOS baseline (RGB SAMA-4B: 55.4 on MeViS). Frozen RGB SAMA-4B with pseudo-RGB renderings or E2VID reconstructions of the same events scores 13.23 and 15.94 $\mathcal{J}\&\mathcal{F}$. Without retraining, Event-SAMA works on real event-camera recordings when the accumulation window matches their event density. A seed-controlled ablation with a measured noise floor of 0.47 $\mathcal{J}\&\mathcal{F}$ finds gains from diverse training sources over MeViS alone (+4.99), RGB-mean event-stem initialisation (+4.33), and a trainable SAM2 mask decoder (+2.76); the event adapter, partial ViT unfreezing and event polarity are within noise at this budget. An earlier, partially converted corpus snapshot trained with the identical recipe scores 1.38 $\mathcal{J}\&\mathcal{F}$ higher on MeViS and 1.12 higher on ReVOS. Both are single runs that also differ in schedule length, so we draw no conclusion about corpus scale.

Title: PairWatch: Pair-Aware Anomaly Transformer for Detecting Adversarial Rendezvous in Public Satellite Catalogs

  • Authors: Young Eun Kwon, Ji Won Yoon
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39679
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39679
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39679]] PairWatch: Pair-Aware Anomaly Transformer for Detecting Adversarial Rendezvous in Public Satellite Catalogs(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39679)
  • Keywords: security, transformer
  • Abstract: Documented adversarial rendezvous and proximity operations (RPO) appear in the public TLE catalog, yet existing TLE-based maneuver detectors treat each satellite in isolation - mistaking routine station-keeping for adversarial behavior and missing RPO entirely when the target stays passive. We argue that the right unit is the satellite pair and the right inductive bias is the kinematic symmetry between approach and recede. We instantiate both with PairWatch, a pair-aware AnomalyTransformer whose attention prior is augmented with a sign-symmetric geometric similarity over relative-motion features, and release an open benchmark of 359 windows from public Space-Track data spanning 11 documented RPO events, 100 Clohessy-Wiltshire synthetic adversarial windows, and a five-class hard-negative set culminating in the TerraSAR-X / TanDEM-X helix formation. Under the deployment-relevant criterion of a single threshold separating real RPO from operational negatives - a necessary property for catalog-scale screening - PairWatch is the only model that succeeds, with the property holding in 100% of bootstrap resamples versus 0-19.5% for three neural baselines and under 0.1% for four physics-informed detectors. A per-source analysis exposes a shortcut-learning failure mode of single-number AUROC: simpler baselines attain higher overall AUROC by shortcutting a synthetic-vs-formation contrast with no operational meaning; removing this single cell flips the test ranking entirely (LSTM-AD), and these baselines fail the security-relevant task the benchmark was built to measure.

Title: Hardware-Aware Model Inversion in Memristive Spiking Neural Networks

  • Authors: Pablo Sorrentino, Fernando M. Quintana, Erika Covi, Fatih Turkmen
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.ET, cs.NE
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39688
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39688
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39688]] Hardware-Aware Model Inversion in Memristive Spiking Neural Networks(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39688)
  • Keywords: privacy, extraction
  • Abstract: Hardware-accelerated spiking neural networks (SNNs) implemented on memristive crossbar arrays offer significant gains in energy efficiency, while device variability and analog noise are sometimes assumed to hinder information extraction. However, whether these hardware effects improve, preserve, or degrade privacy after deployment remains largely unexplored. Therefore, we ask whether model-inversion leakage characterized on an SNN remains representative after the same model is mapped to a non-ideal memristive substrate, and how hardware non-idealities and reliability faults affecting synapses, neuron thresholds, and spike processing reshape that leakage. To study this problem, we introduce Spiking Domain Model Inversion (SDMI), an inversion method that combines Expectation over Transformation (EOT) with surrogate gradients to optimize inputs through stochastic analog behavior and non-differentiable spiking dynamics. We use SDMI consistently across the SNN model, its memristive SNN, and the same memristive SNN under additional hardware perturbations. Across four datasets, reconstruction leakage does not vary monotonically with model accuracy or hardware variability. At comparable accuracy levels, neuron-threshold perturbations increase reconstruction fidelity, measured by SSIM, by up to 51.8%, whereas spike-level perturbations primarily degrade utility without consistently improving reconstruction. Weight noise, in contrast, reduces leakage under the same inversion procedure. These results show that hardware variability is not inherently privacy-preserving: model inversion leakage in memristive SNNs depends on the hardware state and cannot be inferred from model accuracy alone.

Title: Smartphone Indoor Localization Enhanced by Hierarchical Multi-Sensor Context Recognition

  • Authors: Wenhui Feng, Muhan Weng, Jianyu Liu, Xiansheng Yang, Hao Zhang, Liang Wang, Yuan Zhuang
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.RO
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39691
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39691
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39691]] Smartphone Indoor Localization Enhanced by Hierarchical Multi-Sensor Context Recognition(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39691)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Smartphone indoor localization requires low-cost, robust positioning across scene transitions and phone-use variations. Pedestrian dead reckoning (PDR) enables infrastructure-free deployment but accumulates errors from heading drift, structural transitions, and carrying-mode changes. Existing methods often treat contextual cues as a single event stream or local correction signal, limiting their ability to address distinct localization errors. This paper presents a framework enhanced by hierarchical multi-sensor context recognition. A jointly data- and model-driven recognition model predicts environment, scene, and activity probabilities, which are converted into scene-event and action-context segments. Scene-event segments guide scene-constrained PDR correction through corridor heading constraints, landmark attraction, fixed-lag backtracking, and vertical-transition handling. Action-context segments drive action-aware RoNIN-based position compensation for carrying-mode-dependent drift. Experiments on cross-floor and multi-action trajectories against LiDAR-SLAM references show that the proposed framework outperforms PDR and representative learned inertial odometry baselines, demonstrating that hierarchical semantics provide reliable, localization-specific correction cues.

Title: NIF: Normal-Intersection Fields for Geometrically Consistent Oriented Box Refinement

  • Authors: Shaokai Zheng
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39715
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39715
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39715]] NIF: Normal-Intersection Fields for Geometrically Consistent Oriented Box Refinement(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39715)
  • Keywords: fair
  • Abstract: Accurate oriented detection requires a box to follow object boundaries, yet a standard regression head predicts its center, size, and angle from a pooled region representation. Small errors in these coupled parameters can displace long boundaries even when the object is correctly classified. We propose NIF, a refinement module that replaces an additional box regressor with normal-intersection fields. Given a coarse oriented box, the module observes local features at 20 boundary anchors and predicts signed distances along their outward normals. These observations admit an exact affine representation in five geometric variables. A differentiable, reliability-weighted least-squares fit projects the predicted field onto this representation and analytically recovers a refined rectangle. Tangential pooling preserves the normal position profile, while shared projection limits the added network to 0.132M parameters. On DOTA-v1.0, NIF reaches 78.00%, 79.11%, and 79.02% mAP with Oriented R-CNN, LSKNet-S, and Strip R-CNN, respectively, achieving the highest single-scale SOTA result in Table 1. It reaches 68.19% on DIOR-R, improves matched FAIR1M/DIOR-R baselines by 0.30/0.68%, and raises HRSC mean IoU from 0.867 to 0.888 while reducing field error by 24.5%. Across four different detector frameworks with the detectors frozen, relative AP75 improves by 1.57-4.18% and COCO-style mAP by 0.42-3.74%, without detector retraining. Code is available at this https URL.

Title: Cross-Encoder Anchor Refinement for Zero-Shot Anomaly Detection

  • Authors: Kaisong Ji, Ziyang Xu, Chang Bian, Yu Zhou, Wenyu Liu, Xinggang Wang
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39727
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39727
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39727]] Cross-Encoder Anchor Refinement for Zero-Shot Anomaly Detection(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39727)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Zero-shot anomaly detection aims to transfer anomaly discrimination from auxiliary training domains to unseen categories while localizing subtle defects. A key challenge is to construct discriminative references that combine contextual representations with complementary local evidence. We present Cross-Encoder Anchor Refinement (CEAR), which connects two visual encoding paths for the same image at the anchor stage, before normal and anomalous prototypes are constructed. An independent convolutional encoder pretrained for industrial product recognition builds an image-specific memory shared across selected vision transformer (ViT) layers. Spatial anchors read from ViT patch features query this memory through cross-attention and undergo residual refinement. Image-conditioned normal and anomalous queries then gate anchor aggregation to construct prototypes for anomaly scoring. Across 13 industrial and medical datasets, CEAR achieves the best performance in 57 of 68 evaluations spanning image-level detection and pixel-level localization. Notably, CEAR surpasses all other state-of-the-art methods in 41 of 44 pixel-level comparisons. Across 11 localization datasets, mean gains in pixel-level maximum F1 score and average precision are 4.45 and 3.79 percentage points, respectively. This study shows how cross-encoder interaction at the anchor stage can integrate complementary visual representations for fine-grained anomaly localization across domains.

Title: Investigating Data Flows from Android Games

  • Authors: Judit Borcsiczky, Lukas Moog, Boel Nelson
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39730
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39730
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39730]] Investigating Data Flows from Android Games(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39730)
  • Keywords: privacy
  • Abstract: Online applications can share data with arbitrary parties, without users noticing. In practice, these data flows could constitute privacy violations to individual users, with the lack of transparency leaving users unaware of the unwanted data flows. To understand the spread and scale of this privacy threat, we investigate what data is collected and where it is sent in popular free-to-play Android games. As part of our investigation, we conduct a measurement study where we collect and analyze over 16 GB of network traffic from 50 puzzle games. Additionally, we explore the effectiveness of user-side countermeasures for a selection of the apps. We find that while most countermeasures, such as ad-blocking DNS services, decrease data flows, withholding consent and opting out of data collection can produce inconsistent behavior including increased outgoing traffic. To encourage further analysis, we release our measurements as an open dataset.

Title: When the Top Answer Decides: Gender Bias in Decision Models

  • Authors: Yeb Havinga
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.CY
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39747
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39747
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39747]] When the Top Answer Decides: Gender Bias in Decision Models(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39747)
  • Keywords: fair
  • Abstract: Decision models are language models built to route, classify and triage: they return option probabilities from one forward pass, and an application acts on the top answer. They are offered off the shelf, trained for the decision format rather than for fairness or any one application, and we found no bias measurement in their cards. We test TypeSafe's closed Jev and five open decision models on gender-swapped yes/no decisions about people (Tamkin et al., 2023), BBQ, StereoSet and the gender-bias studies of Fulgu and Capraro (2024). Five of the six give women significantly more favourable answers than men (+0.05 to +0.10 in log-odds), and a gender swap changes 1-3.5% of top answers (Jev's partly through its call-to-call variation). The makers' recommended confidence gate removes many biased commitments but raises the stereotyped share of those it keeps in ambiguous BBQ gender questions (granite-switch 0.76 to 0.85, pplx 0.81 to 1.00). Probes show gender preferences in the same models: all six rate violence for a greater good as more acceptable against a man than against a woman, and two default to "girl" when forced to guess a child's gender without a cue. Decision-level and probability-level measures differ, so audits should read a model as its application does.

Title: SEAL: Sealed Queries for Local Sanitization against Indirect Prompt Injection

  • Authors: Doohyun Kim, Donghwa Kang, Mingyu Jun, Brent ByungHoon Kang
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39758
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39758
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39758]] SEAL: Sealed Queries for Local Sanitization against Indirect Prompt Injection(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39758)
  • Keywords: defense, attack
  • Abstract: Indirect prompt injection threatens local LLM agents through untrusted tool outputs, motivating defenses that suppress attacks while preserving task evidence under limited inference budgets. We introduce SEAL, which learns a soft prompt and scoring readout offline to sanitize tool outputs using a frozen task model. SEAL constructs scoring queries from trusted initial context and the learned prompt, keeping the reference independent of inspected tool text. It scores tokens without normalization across context tokens and uses preservation regularization to encourage retention of useful evidence during localized deletion. This design requires neither defense-generated tokens nor a separate verifier model. Across AgentDojo and AgentDyn, SEAL suppresses attacks while improving task completion under attack. Specifically, on AgentDojo, SEAL reduces attack success from 34.6\% to 0.1\% and increases Joint---task completion without attack success---from 51.4\% to 77.5\%, while using approximately $127\times$ fewer defense-related tokens than PISanitizer, a state-of-the-art prompt injection defense. Replication on a second backbone with the same training recipe supports applicability across model families.

Title: Multi-output LSTM soft sensors for industrial anaerobic co-digestion using LSTM-beta-VAE generative data augmentation

  • Authors: Rohit Murali, Benaissa Dekhici, Tao Chen, Dongda Zhang, Michael Short
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI, eess.SY
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39768
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39768
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39768]] Multi-output LSTM soft sensors for industrial anaerobic co-digestion using LSTM-beta-VAE generative data augmentation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39768)
  • Keywords: robust, generative
  • Abstract: Anaerobic digestion (AD) requires close monitoring of key process variables, including pH, the ratio of volatile organic acids to alkaline buffer capacity and biogas production, to maintain biological stability and prevent process failure. While mechanistic frameworks such as the Anaerobic Digestion Model No. 1 are highly parameterised, computationally expensive, and difficult to calibrate for real-time applications, data-driven Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM) networks offer a potentially more adaptable alternative. However, current data-driven approaches in AD are predominantly limited to single-output architectures that focus solely on production yields, as opposed to simultaneous prediction of vital stability indicators. Furthermore, industrial AD datasets are typically sparse and skewed toward steady-state operations. Baseline evaluations demonstrated this scarcity prevents standard algorithms (Random Forest, XGBoost) and unaugmented LSTMs from recognising the transition to unstable operations, resulting in poor estimation of stability indicators. To overcome these challenges, this study proposes a novel generative data augmentation framework using an LSTM-beta-Variational Autoencoder coupled with a multi-output LSTM (MO-LSTM) soft sensor. By synthesising physically valid sequences of these transitions to unstable operations from one year of full-scale plant data, the method safely populates sparse regions of the feature space. Trained on this augmented dataset, the MO-LSTM achieved robust simultaneous estimation across all targets. This approach mitigates data sparsity, providing a basis for hybrid mechanistic-data-driven modelling and early-warning soft sensors in industrial bioprocesses.

Title: Quantum-Inspired Convolutional Networks for High-Resolution Pseudo-Label Generation from Multimodal Earth Observation Data

  • Keywords: robust, extraction
  • Abstract: Accurate land-cover mapping from multimodal Earth observation (EO) data remains a challenge in regions where high-resolution annotations are scarce or costly to obtain. This work introduces a quantum convolutional encoder-decoder network for pseudo-label generation using Sentinel-1 SAR and Sentinel-2 multispectral imagery, guided only by coarse MODIS-derived land-cover maps. The proposed framework uses quantum-inspired convolutional blocks to enhance feature extraction and cross-sensor fusion, enabling the model to infer a fine-scale semantic structure from heterogeneous inputs. Experiments were conducted on the DFC2020 dataset, which provides a quadruple of Sentinel-1 SAR, Sentinel-2 multispectral, low-resolution MODIS labels and high-resolution reference land-cover maps. These pseudo-labels are subsequently evaluated through classification metrics on the test data, demonstrating that the proposed approach produces more spatially coherent and class-consistent compared to classical baselines. The results highlight the potential of quantum-inspired deep learning to improve label generation in low-annotation remote sensing scenarios, offering a scalable path towards high-quality land-cover products for EO applications.

Title: SCOPE: Semantic COdec Pre-Editing for Object-Preserving Compression

  • Authors: Silvia Dani, Leonardo Galteri, Marco Bertini
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39778
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39778
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39778]] SCOPE: Semantic COdec Pre-Editing for Object-Preserving Compression(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39778)
  • Keywords: protect
  • Abstract: Image compression is increasingly used before machine vision systems, yet standard codecs are mainly optimized for human perception rather than downstream task accuracy. We propose SCOPE (Semantic COdec Pre-Editing), a conditional image pre-editor that modifies images before compression to improve the bitrate and detection accuracy trade-off. The model is conditioned on the codec family and compression severity, allowing a single trained network to adapt to multiple compression settings without retraining or loading codec-specific weights. Instead of uniformly preserving the whole image, the pre-editor learns to protect task-relevant regions while simplifying less important background content to improve compressibility. The resulting rate-accuracy curves show consistent gains across different codec families and detector architectures, together with strong transferability to unseen learned and wavelet-based compression families.

Title: AI at a Turning Point: From ChatGPT through Neural Networks to Neuromorphic AI

  • Authors: Volker Kempe
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.NE
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39796
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39796
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39796]] AI at a Turning Point: From ChatGPT through Neural Networks to Neuromorphic AI(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39796)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: The tension between the established technologies of connectionist AI and their neuromorphic challengers is attracting growing attention, above all because of the enormous energy demand of the former and the low power consumption of the latter. Developing and running large AI models consumes gigawatt- to terawatt-hours of energy, while the human brain operates on a continuous 20 watts. Spiking neural networks (SNNs) counter this energy demand with a frugal approach and bring elements of self-organization into the methodological repertoire of AI: local learning instead of global error backpropagation, event-driven computing, and time as a carrier of information. They do not replace today's AI but extend it; in hybrid systems each approach can be deployed where its strengths lie. This monograph explains the field in a generally accessible yet mathematically rigorous way. Using the text-generation module of ChatGPT as an example, the principle, elegance, and computational cost of transformer-based AI are presented and compared with the coding, learning, and network-design methods of SNNs. Throughout, relevant mechanisms of the human nervous system serve as model and reference point. Topics include spike-timing-dependent plasticity, population and temporal coding, the Neural Engineering Framework, neuromorphic hardware from Loihi 2 to commercial edge processors, and the prospects for classical and neuromorphic AI to complement and extend each other.

Title: InferD: A Honeypot Framework for AI Inference Infrastructure

  • Authors: Hicham Hammouchi, Gabriele Lenzini
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39807
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39807
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39807]] InferD: A Honeypot Framework for AI Inference Infrastructure(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39807)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Large language model serving stacks, commercial inference APIs, vector databases, and agent orchestration gateways are now ex- posed on the public internet and indexed by commodity scanners within hours of deployment. The threat characteristics of this sur- face are only beginning to be measured, and the honeypot frame- works that could measure it were designed for conventional net- work services rather than for AI infrastructure. In fact, emulating an AI service is a distinct deception problem: the observable behavior of the service, including model inventories, token formats, tool-call protocols, and authorization flows, must be reproduced faithfully, while the underlying computation, model inference and tool ex- ecution, must never occur. We present InferD, a low-interaction honeypot framework organized around a single principle we call deception without inference: every request an adversary can observe is answered from static response pools, and nothing an adversary submits is ever executed. InferD decomposes the emulation into four deception planes, transport, service protocol, vulnerability surface, and agentic session, adds a provenance plane that links a planted credential to evidence of downstream model-mediated processing.

Title: Possibility-based Inference: A Deterministic Framework for Robust and Reliable Uncertainty Quantification in Decision-Making

  • Authors: Seyedfakhredin Musavishavazi, Maryam Alipour
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39821
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39821
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39821]] Possibility-based Inference: A Deterministic Framework for Robust and Reliable Uncertainty Quantification in Decision-Making(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39821)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Decision-making under uncertainty remains a key issue in Machine Learning (ML), particularly in the deployment of safety-critical systems, where reliable quantification of system behavior is essential for evaluating behavioral impacts and strengthening confidence in ML models. This contribution, influenced by fuzzy set theory and grounded in Bayesian statistics, addresses challenges related to data precision and the performance-accuracy trade-off in Bayesian inference (BI). To this end, we introduce a possibility-based contrastive grade of prediction as an alternative quantification method to existing latent posterior-based approaches for parameter estimation (approximation) and model selection. Unlike conventional Bayesian inference, which represents uncertainty within the probabilistic framework, the proposed model adopts a possibility-based representation that allows uncertain and potentially conflicting information to be characterized more flexibly. Rather than focusing on isolated aspects of a problem, the resulting mathematical framework provides a systematic approach to data mining while mitigating overly confident, yet implausible predictions generated by highly specialized deep learning architectures.

Title: Exploring the Potential of Symbolic Regression in Precision Livestock Farming

  • Authors: Elisabeth Mayrhuber, Maciej Oczak, Michael Iwersen, David Brunner, Viktoria Dorfer, Stephan Winkler
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39839
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39839
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39839]] Exploring the Potential of Symbolic Regression in Precision Livestock Farming(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39839)
  • Keywords: interpretability, explainability
  • Abstract: The ability to predict farrowing time in pigs is crucial for optimal farm management and ensuring piglet survival. This study investigates the potential of explainable artificial intelligence for precision livestock farming, focusing on acceleration sensor data from sows around the time of farrowing. Accurately predicting farrowing allows farmers to balance both, piglet safety by avoiding piglet crushing events and the mother sow's welfare, by avoiding early crating. Traditional machine learning models often lack interpretability (``closed-box'' problem). Explainable artificial intelligence offers a solution by revealing the decision-making processes within the models. This work is split into two stages. First the detection of nest building behaviour and second the prediction of the remaining time to farrowing. The nest building detection was possible for 166 out of 180 sows and successful within 48 hours before farrowing for 149 sows. The nest building detection is purely based on the change of a sow's activity level and uses no machine learning techniques. Further, this study utilizes symbolic regression, an open-box modelling technique that generates interpretable mathematical formulas, to predict the remaining time until the onset of farrowing. The results are compared with closed-box models, like support vector regression and artificial neural networks, for both explainability and performance. Notably, symbolic regression achieved a mean absolute error of 9.28 hours for the time-to-farrowing prediction, outperforming the closed-box models in terms of performance and interpretability. This research demonstrates that explainable artificial intelligence, specifically symbolic regression, can offer both interpretable results and competitive performance in precision livestock farming.

Title: Surprise Alone Learns Lies: Evidence-Gated Continual Learning for LLMs from Streaming Claims

  • Authors: Abdelkader Metales, Miriam Grabenheinrich
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.CL, cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39840
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39840
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39840]] Surprise Alone Learns Lies: Evidence-Gated Continual Learning for LLMs from Streaming Claims(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39840)
  • Keywords: attack, large language model
  • Abstract: Continual learning methods for large language models increasingly decide what to learn by surprise, keeping the inputs the model finds least predictable. This rule is unsafe when the stream contains lies. A planted falsehood about something the model knows has low probability under the model's belief, so it is highly surprising by construction, and a surprise-driven learner absorbs nearly every such lie, at a rate similar to random selection with the same budget. We study the problem with LoRA-adapted students (Qwen2.5-1.5B, Qwen2.5-3B and Llama-3.2-3B) that learn from a stream of true and false claims about invented people and real CounterFact facts. We report four findings. (1) A verifier key, a judge that must approve each claim, removes lies to the extent that its verifier is accurate; real LLM judges are cautious and reject unsourced truths and even source-backed updates. (2) Calibrated regression tests on unrelated knowledge slow learning down without filtering out lies. (3) The original model's own prior flags claims that contradict what it knows, without a judge. It separates lies about known facts from true new facts (AUC 0.78-0.83 across three models) but not from legitimate updates of the same kind of fact (AUC 0.53). It saves 43% of judge calls, but an attacker with access to the model can choose lies that pass it. (4) If trusted sources exist that an attacker cannot plant, routing contradicted claims to evidence recovers legitimate updates and the combined system has the lowest poison rate among learned claims; when an attacker plants matching sources for 30% of the lies, the system ends up worse than the judge alone. The acceptance signals answer different questions, and none of them decides on its own whether a claim is true.

Title: LiDAR-SHIELD: A Fully Dockerized Adversarial Robustness Assessment Framework for LiDAR-Based 3D Perception Models

  • Authors: Alexandros Gkillas, Ioulia Kapsali, Christos Anagnostopoulos, Aris S. Lalos
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39848
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39848
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39848]] LiDAR-SHIELD: A Fully Dockerized Adversarial Robustness Assessment Framework for LiDAR-Based 3D Perception Models(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39848)
  • Keywords: attack, robust, segmentation
  • Abstract: LiDAR-based 3D perception underpins autonomous driving and robotics, yet deploying these models safely requires stress-testing them against adversarial inputs before real-world use. Although state-of-the-art adversarial attacks exist, their implementations are often fragmented across individual repositories, each tied to a specific architecture or task and requiring manual environment reconstruction and model-specific integration code. We present LIDARSHIELD, a unified and fully Dockerized framework that reverses this workflow: rather than installing the target model inside the toolkit, LIDARSHIELD exposes any model through three lightweight HTTP endpoints, namely /info, /forward, and /gradient As a result, connecting a new model or dataset requires only a single loader function or adapter. The framework supports both 3D semantic segmentation and 3D object detection through the same engine-service protocol, integrating a task-aware suite of state-of-the-art attacks, including PGD and Square Attack for segmentation, and point perturbation, point removal, point injection, and LiDAttack for detection. This enables systematic exposure of corner cases and vulnerabilities across heterogeneous LiDAR backbones. The LiDAR-Shield codebase is publicly available at this https URL.

Title: Integrating Perception, Comprehension, and Projection for Alert Validation: A Unified Cyber Situational Awareness Framework

  • Authors: Navodika Karunasingha, Mohan Baruwal Chhetri, Surya Nepal, Cecile Paris, Salil S. Kanhere
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39849
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39849
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39849]] Integrating Perception, Comprehension, and Projection for Alert Validation: A Unified Cyber Situational Awareness Framework(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39849)
  • Keywords: security, attack
  • Abstract: Security Operation Centres (SOCs) must validate large volumes of alerts with limited analyst resources, yet alerts often lack sufficient context to determine whether suspicious activity is attack related. Overall, richer CSA does not necessarily lead to better alert validation. Title: DWISE: Denoising Workload-Informed Scheduling Engine

  • Authors: Lie Li, Wen Li, Junxiao Shen, Jindong Gu, Guosheng Hu
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39852
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39852
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39852]] DWISE: Denoising Workload-Informed Scheduling Engine(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39852)
  • Keywords: diffusion, large language model
  • Abstract: Unlike autoregressive large language models (AR-LLMs), diffusion LLMs (dLLMs) generate text by iteratively denoising many tokens in parallel, offering a promising path to efficient inference. Existing serving frameworks, however, schedule dLLM requests with policies designed for AR models, namely first-come-first-served (FCFS) admission and prefill priority. We find that both policies are ill-suited to dLLMs, wasting much of their speed. FCFS ignores that a dLLM request's decoding cost is highly heterogeneous and unknown at arrival, causing severe head-of-line blocking. Prefill priority, widely used in AR-LLM serving to keep time-to-first-token low, sharply cuts dLLM efficiency, as rounds that run only prompts leave most of the batch idle. We present DWISE, a Denoising Workload-Informed Scheduling Engine for efficient dLLM serving. DWISE schedules requests by their remaining work, measured in a block-level unit shared by prefill and denoising, so both phases share a round. To predict the unknown denoising workload, DWISE combines a lightweight prompt-based predictor at admission with a single refinement from the first denoising round's hidden states, which capture model-specific information absent from the prompt and require no additional model execution. Implemented in SGLang and evaluated on five models from three dLLM families under bursty workloads, DWISE reduces mean end-to-end latency over SGLang by 5.4$\times$ on average and up to 14.9$\times$ while preserving task accuracy. The code will be released publicly.

Title: KV Cache Quantization for Diffusion Language Models

  • Authors: Yan Tai, Yunan Ding, Guangzhao Li, Siqi Luo, Chaoyang Zhang, Guangtao Zhai, Guodong Guo, Xiaohong Liu

  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: KV cache quantization is well studied for autoregressive language models, where cached prefix states are exact under causal attention and quantization is mainly a cache reconstruction problem. Diffusion language models (dLLMs) break this assumption. Fast dLLM inference reuses KV states across denoising steps, but bidirectional token representations can change as masked tokens are revealed, so the reused cache may already differ from full recomputation before quantization is applied. We study KV cache quantization under this cache-reuse error. We adapt KIVI and KVQuant to Fast-dLLM-style prefix and dual cache reuse, and introduce TRACE and dLLM-KVQ. TRACE reconstructs feasible dLLM reveal trajectories from prompt-answer pairs, providing calibration states that match iterative masked decoding. dLLM-KVQ uses these trajectories to choose per-layer K-cache entries that remain in high precision according to output- and attention-level behavior metrics, while applying standard low-bit value quantization. Across LLaDA and Dream evaluations on GSM8K, HumanEval, MATH, and MBPP, dLLM-KVQ achieves the highest average score among quantized methods across the reported 4-bit and 2-bit prefix/dual cache settings.

Title: Attention, Dynamic Wave and Hybrid Sequence Mixers in Language Models

  • Authors: Dmytro Babarytskyi, Oleksandr Babarytskyi
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39854
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39854
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39854]] Attention, Dynamic Wave and Hybrid Sequence Mixers in Language Models(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39854)
  • Keywords: transformer, large language model
  • Abstract: Large Language Models (LLMs) rely on Multi-Head Attention (MHA) for sequence modeling, but scaling to long contexts is constrained by the quadratic cost of attention, motivating sub-quadratic recurrent alternatives. This paper formalizes a Dynamic Wave recurrent layer, based on input-dependent exponential damping and cosine phase modulation, and evaluates three 10.94M-parameter configurations (Pure Transformer, Pure Wave, and Hybrid Wave-Attention) on TinyStories under perplexity, latency, throughput and peak VRAM profiling up to 4096 tokens. The Hybrid configuration reaches the best perplexity, 5.95 on the full held-out split against 6.93 for the Transformer and 7.26 for Pure Wave. The efficiency picture, however, is governed by implementation rather than by architecture. Against an unfused attention baseline, Pure Wave uses 78.7% less peak VRAM at a 4096-token context (1404.3 MB vs. 6601.6 MB); against a fused scaled-dot-product attention kernel the same comparison yields no advantage at all (1404.3 MB vs. 1403.6 MB), and in training mode Pure Wave is 8.3% more expensive than the Transformer. Likewise, hoisting elementwise terms out of the recurrence loop speeds the layer up 2.53-fold with no custom kernel, reducing its latency gap to attention from 2.48-fold to 1.12-fold. A hybrid stack is found to inherit the cost of its more expensive mixer rather than the advantage of its cheaper one, its peak VRAM matching the Pure Transformer to within 0.1%. A closed-form memory model reproduces these measurements and predicts the context length beyond which attention overtakes the vocabulary projection. The findings indicate that reported efficiency gains for recurrent attention replacements must be stated relative to a named attention backend and a named implementation, and that hybridization is best justified on quality grounds.

Title: Syndrome Trellis Decryption in Post-Quantum Cryptography

  • Authors: Meir Ariel
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39873
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39873
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39873]] Syndrome Trellis Decryption in Post-Quantum Cryptography(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39873)
  • Keywords: security, attack, robust
  • Abstract: We present a novel post-quantum cryptographic framework based on masked high-memory convolutional codes in the Niederreiter paradigm. The public key is derived from a high-memory polynomial parity-check matrix augmented by dense random masking and additional invertible linear transformations. The resulting matrix exhibits strong random-like properties, effectively concealing its algebraic structure and resisting known structural and algebraic attacks. Legitimate recipients retain polynomial-time decoding capability, whereas adversaries appear to be limited to generic information-set decoding with exponential complexity. Under standard cryptanalytic estimates, the proposed construction achieves security margins exceeding those of classical McEliece- and Niederreiter-based systems by factors greater than 2^200. Beyond its enhanced security profile, the construction offers considerable design flexibility, supporting arbitrary plaintext lengths, linear-time decryption, and a uniform per-bit computational cost, enabling efficient scalability to very long messages. Practical implementation is facilitated by a parallel array of syndrome-trellis decoders, dynamically instantiated according to candidate syndrome values, enabling efficient hardware and software implementations with high throughput. Overall, the proposed scheme is a promising candidate for robust, scalable, quantum-resistant public-key cryptography.

Title: What Survives Label Downsampling? Exact Region Survival for Coarse Segmentation Targets

  • Authors: Zhiyuan Zhang, Yukun Ou, San Tek Choi
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39880
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39880
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39880]] What Survives Label Downsampling? Exact Region Survival for Coarse Segmentation Targets(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39880)
  • Keywords: segmentation
  • Abstract: Segmentation networks that predict at an output stride are trained against label maps downsampled by strided sampling, nearest-neighbor resampling or majority pooling. These methods label each coarse grid cell on its own, so a region that wins no cell is absent from the target and receives no positive label. We present MatchDown, which builds the coarse target from a minimum-cost matching between regions and the grid cells they occupy. It keeps as many regions as any one-region-per-cell target can, certifies how many losses are unavoidable, and with priority tiers (MatchDown-P) keeps every evaluated region (a region that a metric scores) of at least one cell's area. On COCO, Cityscapes and BBBC038 at factors 2 to 16, MatchDown-P loses exactly the minimum number of regions, whereas the downsampling used by StarDist, nnU-Net and connectomics pyramids loses four to five times as many on COCO at factor 8. Keeping a region in the target and reconstructing its shape are different problems: we show that no region lost by majority pooling can be reconstructed above IoU 0.5 by upsampling any coarse target, yet on BBBC038 a star-convex head trained on MatchDown targets segments 35% of the nuclei that majority pooling loses above IoU 0.5, against 15% when trained on majority targets and 24% on StarDist's strided target. On two nucleus datasets, with thresholds cross-validated for count error, this head has instance accuracy at IoU 0.5 (Acc50) 2.6 to 4.2 points above the strided target and at most 2.8 points below cell-center sampling, and a lower count error than the strided and cell-center targets on BBBC039. On BBBC039, an affinity head, which outputs one label per cell, lowers count error relative to majority targets by 9.3 nuclei per image at 7.2 points lower Acc50. MatchDown runs once, before training, in place of the label-downsampling step.

  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.LG

  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39889]] sDMD: Sinkhorn Distribution Matching Distillation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39889)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Distribution Matching Distillation (DMD) distills multi-step diffusion models into few-step generators by minimizing reverse KL divergence. Despite its strong performance, DMD guides samples by local density alone, which introduces two limitations: teacher scores can be unreliable in regions of low teacher density, and the mode-seeking behavior of reverse KL can collapse the student onto a subset of the teacher modes. We first connect DMD to Wasserstein gradient flow, showing that its gradient follows from a fixed-point formulation with integral KL (IKL) as the energy functional. This perspective motivates augmenting IKL with an optimal transport term that addresses these limitations by guiding outlier samples toward the target and promoting mode coverage. We realize this term using Sinkhorn divergence estimated from finite batches. In practice, however, errors in the estimated Sinkhorn velocity can perturb in-domain samples for which IKL guidance is already accurate. To this end, we introduce sDMD, which adaptively activates the Sinkhorn correction based on discriminator outputs, focusing on outlier samples where IKL may be unreliable. With 2--4 sampling steps on FLUX.1-dev, Qwen-Image-20B, and Wan2.1-14B, sDMD outperforms distillation baselines on GenEval, DPG, WISE, and VBench while improving sample diversity over DMD2.

A practical difficulty arising in the numerical approximation of Koopman operators is the choice of a ``dictionary'', meaning an effective finite-dimensional representation of the function space.   • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39910

  • Keywords: attack
  • Abstract: Deterministic variants of CSIDH remove randomness from the group action, either through implementation techniques or, as in Houben's deterministic CSIDH and OSIDH-LD, by design. Since earlier fault attacks on CSIDH mostly target random point sampling, determinism seems to close that avenue. We show that it opens another: every secret-dependent step occurs at a fixed, public point of every execution, so an attacker can fault the same step repeatedly. Our attack forces the mask that selects which of two lanes computes the next isogeny to zero, for example by skipping one instruction. Whether the output changes reveals whether a secret exponent reaches a chosen threshold, and a binary search over these thresholds recovers the full key with one fault per execution: at most 297 oracle queries for OSIDH-LD and 222 for deterministic CSIDH with CSIDH-512-sized keys. We recover full OSIDH-LD keys in a software fault simulation of the reference implementation, and show on a ChipWhisperer-Lite that the targeted instruction is skipped with probability at least 0.94 at an isolated fault position. Finally, we argue that fault resistance requires giving up determinism, and propose countermeasures relying on randomization.

Rare strings become tokens only when enough corpus frequency pushes them over the merge threshold. This suggests that membership signal should appear in rare tokens used by the candidate dataset, rather than in the order in which merges were made. We instantiate this view as RTAM-Fusion, which calibrates rare-token mass with shadow tokenizers and combines signals across rarity levels. Existing defenses also continue to leak membership signal against adaptive attackers at production vocabulary sizes. Code: this https URL

  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39923

  • Abstract: As language models (LMs) have improved, an increasingly common means of evaluating their linguistic competence is through explicit prompting (e.g., "Which sentence is more acceptable?"). We test this by replacing standard focus markers with randomly generated ones; token probabilities still beat chance but show significantly greater performance decrease compared to prompting. These results suggest that metalinguistic prompting, while non-trivial for LMs, can also be more robust when testing stylistically atypical data. We argue that this has important consequences for the assessment of LMs' language competence, even if LMs do not perform metalinguistic judgments through human-like introspection.

To bridge this gap, we propose \textbf{Hierarchical Hint-based Reward}, a framework that leverages the intrinsic sub-question structure of mathematical problems as structured intermediate conditioning signals during RLVR training. Unlike chain-of-thought prompting, these hierarchical hints serve as logically grounded intermediate milestones that effectively reduce the reasoning horizon toward the final answer. To validate this framework, we introduce \textbf{HiMath}, a benchmark of hierarchically structured mathematical problems. Across diverse LLMs, hierarchical hints consistently improve reasoning performance even without task-specific training. When integrated into RL training via H$^2$R, they boost Qwen2.5-Math-7B accuracy on HiMath from 30.4\% to 39.1\% using hierarchical hints, and up to 45.6\% with Oracle Hierarchical Hints. This pronounced improvement demonstrates that incorporating reliable intermediate checkpoints into reward design is essential for sustaining complex mathematical reasoning.

Title: MLLM-GS: Multimodal Large Language Model Prior-Guided Fast Novel View Synthesis in Extreme Environments

The latter improves 3DGS efficiency by optimizing rasterization, Gaussian memory layout, and parameter updates, reducing redundant computation, memory-access overhead, and optimization costs. Extensive experiments on multiple degraded scenes demonstrate that MLLM-GS effectively handles various severe degradations and achieves high-quality NVS with significantly lower reconstruction time and memory consumption. Title: BiHashFormer: Hash-Driven Dual-Branch Transformer for Efficient Object Detection in HRW Shots

  • Authors: Jingchen Huang, Wenxi Li, Chenyang Lyu, Moran Liu, Haozhe Lin, Yuchen Guo
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39937
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39937
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39937]] BiHashFormer: Hash-Driven Dual-Branch Transformer for Efficient Object Detection in HRW Shots(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39937)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Recent advances in gigapixel-level imaging have brought High-Resolution Wide shots to the forefront of research. However, these images present significant challenges: extreme sparsity of foreground, gigapixel-level resolutions and interleaving of foreground and background. This causes traditional detectors and attention mechanisms to be hindered by background, resulting in inefficiency and inaccuracy. To tackle this problem, we propose BiHashFormer, a dual sparsified branch transformer built on hashing. RoI Selector will predict the foreground proportion for top-k selecting target-containing windows. The compression branch processes windows selected and refines attention into HashAttention by discarding half of the key-value pairs, reducing cost and improving efficiency. The compensation branch uses HashMiner to perform low-cost hash searches in the remaining regions, recovering features of missed objects. Experiments confirm that the selection relationship in HashAttention is one-way and reveal the preference of different queries for keys. In experiments on the gigapixel benchmark PANDA, BiHashFormer reduces 37.5% backbone FLOPs while improving AP50 to 81.0%. Moreover, this parallel dual-branch structure allows the model to avoid inference delays caused by inter-branch dependencies, while achieving much greater stability than single-branch models, specially with low rates of RoI selection.

  • Abstract: Recent advances in gigapixel-level imaging have brought High-Resolution Wide shots to the forefront of research. This makes traditional close-up detectors inaccurate and slow as they are overwhelmed by the background. Although previous research has explored sparse backbones, their fixed sparsity patterns lack the adaptability required to handle diverse target numbers. To address this, we introduce ElasticFormer, a sparse backbone that dynamically allocates computational resources based on foreground proportion. After scoring windows based on variance, proposed ElasticSelector module will predict the foreground proportion for top-k selection. The mechanism guides the model to select target-containing windows, scaling resources in areas where objects are clustered. We introduce a novel loss function combined with the 3-phase training strategy for ElasticSelector, allowing it to function properly when bounding box annotations are missing. A WSOD study is carried on PASCAL VOC 2007 to evaluate its extensibility. Further, ElasticNet is created to verify its backbone-agnostic nature. In experiments on the PANDA gigapixel benchmark, ElasticFormer reduces backbone FLOPs by 80% while achieving a significant improvement in AP50 when compared to fixed-ratio sparse methods.

Title: UniRA: Unified Representation Alignment and Auto-Encoding for Efficient Diffusion Generation

  • Authors: An Lanji, Dawei Liu, Jin Li, Haoran Xu, Mei Chen, Yu Tian
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39948
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39948
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39948]] UniRA: Unified Representation Alignment and Auto-Encoding for Efficient Diffusion Generation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39948)
  • Keywords: diffusion, transformer
  • Abstract: Diffusion transformers (DiTs) have become the dominant architecture for high-fidelity image synthesis, yet they suffer from slow training convergence and high computational cost. Two recent lines of work address this from complementary angles: Representation Alignment (REPA) accelerates DiT training by aligning intermediate features with a pretrained external representation model (e.g., DINOv2), while Representation Auto-Encoders (RAE) replace the pixel-space VAE with a compact representation-space tokenizer learned from self-supervised features. However, these approaches are developed and applied in isolation: REPA still relies on a pixel-space VAE for latent diffusion, and RAE does not exploit the alignment signal that accelerates the diffusion backbone. We present \textbf{UniRA}, a unified framework that couples representation alignment and representation auto-encoding into a single objective. UniRA learns a representation-space tokenizer whose output serves simultaneously as the diffusion target and the alignment anchor, so the tokenizer, the diffusion backbone, and the external representation model are co-optimized. We give a theoretical result showing that this joint optimization preserves the representation quality of the external model while accelerating diffusion convergence, and we derive a principled weighting that balances the reconstruction, alignment, and generation objectives. On ImageNet class-conditional generation at 256*256 and 512*512, UniRA matches or surpasses state-of-the-art DiTs (SD3, REPA, RAE) in FID while reducing training steps by up to 5*. We further show that the unified representation space supports improved zero-shot classification and linear probing, confirming that generation and understanding benefit from a shared, aligned latent space.

Title: When to spend privacy: optimal allocation in private learning

  • Authors: Umberto Biccari, Yongcun Song
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI, math.OC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39954
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39954
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.39954]] When to spend privacy: optimal allocation in private learning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39954)
  • Keywords: privacy
  • Abstract: Privacy accounting bounds the cumulative privacy cost of Differentially Private Stochastic Gradient Descent but does not determine how to allocate a fixed budget across iterations. We formulate the complete Gaussian noise-variance schedule as an open-loop control under a composed zero-Concentrated Differential Privacy constraint, explicitly accounting for schedule selection. For expected quadratic terminal tracking, we prove that every local minimizer exhausts the certified budget, yielding an exact-budget simplex formulation. We introduce Privacy-Budget Adjoint Mirror Descent (PB-AMD), combining pathwise adjoint gradients with entropic updates that preserve positivity, charge floors, and the prescribed budget. Under regularity and coercivity assumptions, we establish existence and budget stability of optimal schedules, design-to-training utility stability, and deterministic, stochastic, and sample-average stationarity guarantees. A linear-quadratic reduction yields an explicit square-root allocation law. Experiments demonstrate tracking improvements over several allocation rules and predictive improvements over uniform allocation in a learning-rate restart setting, where learning-rate allocation performs similarly. Comparisons at a common stationarity tolerance show lower schedule-optimization times than projected gradient descent on the tested problems. The framework connects certified privacy accounting with optimization through nonlinear training dynamics.

Title: Upcycling Language Models for Multiple Specialized Domains

  • Authors: Mingzi Cao, Xi Wang, Nikolaos Aletras
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40091
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40091
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.40091]] Upcycling Language Models for Multiple Specialized Domains(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40091)
  • Keywords: transformer, large language model
  • Abstract: Effective multi-domain adaptation of large language models requires prioritizing relevant domain knowledge while minimizing interference from irrelevant domains. Existing approaches commonly use Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) to train a separate adapter for each domain and combine these adapters within one model. However, they either weight adapters equally regardless of the input domain or dynamically weight adapters while still activating all of them including irrelevant ones. Additionally, while efficient, their low-rank structure limits performance. In contrast, upcycling is a model expansion method that adds experts and a router to a dense Transformer layer offering larger capacity than LoRA approaches. In this paper, we propose Domain-Specialized Upcycling (DS-Upcycling) for multi-domain adaptation. We introduce a new domain-aware supervision that allows the router to effectively learn to distinguish domains by selecting the appropriate experts. During fine-tuning on each new domain, we update only the corresponding expert and router while freezing all other parameters. Experiments show that our method achieves the highest average performance across the adapted domains compared to baselines while retaining general performance. With just 256 examples per domain for router supervision, DS-Upcycling achieves 90-98% routing accuracy after adaptation compared with 43-70% for routers trained without our approach.

The radiology report accompanies the scan and localizes disease at the suborgan level, but existing text-promptable models condition on short referring phrases, and most operate in 2D. We present RepConTUR, a single native-3D early-fusion multimodal model that conditions volumetric abdominal tumor segmentation on the narrative report. We extend AbdomenAtlas 3.0 to a suborgan-level promptable segmentation dataset and generate report-based VQA pairs, yielding four report-derived tasks on the same annotations: referring segmentation (RES), report-grounded segmentation (GSEG), report generation (RRG), and visual question answering (VQA). We train RepConTUR, comprising a 3D CT encoder, an early-fusion language model, and a native-3D segmentation module, jointly on the four tasks. Across liver, kidney and pancreas, RepConTUR attains the highest Dice similarity coefficient (DSC), instance-wise precision, and detection F1 among the text-promptable baselines, and adding the report raises mean precision while leaving recall comparable. Against two vision-only specialists, nnU-Net and the report-supervised R-Super, RepConTUR attains the highest value on every in-domain metric for liver and pancreas and the highest mean DSC, recall, precision, and detection F1 over the four out-of-distribution public cohorts. Ablations indicate that conditioning on the narrative report raises detection F1 from 0.499 to 0.528 within the same model, that joint GSEG training raises RES F1 from 0.450 to 0.482, and that the native-3D segmentation module raises GSEG F1 from 0.139 to 0.528 over its 2D counterpart. Code is available at this https URL.

Title: Coupled Guidance for Flow Matching

  • Authors: Mateo Clémente, Léo Maxime Brunswic, Rui Heng Yang, Amir Rasouli
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.RO
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40109
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40109
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.40109]] Coupled Guidance for Flow Matching(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40109)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Guidance methods are essential tools for steering the generation process in diffusion models, improving the generalizability of stochastic robotic controllers and enabling high-quality controlled image synthesis. Despite strong parallels between flow-matching and diffusion, integrating guidance into flow-matching models remains quite challenging. Recent work has proposed general guidance frameworks for flow-matching, however, they are direct adaptations of diffusion guidance methods and can prove unstable. In this work, we conduct a theoretical analysis of guidance in flow-matching and show that a naive translation of diffusion-based guidance to flow-matching breaks the coupling distribution used during training, resulting in weaker control over sampling accuracy. Building on this insight, we propose Coupled Guidance, a flow-matching-specific guidance mechanism derived from a geometric perspective that leverages the coupling structure inherent to flow-matching training. To validate the effectiveness of our approach, we conduct extensive experimentation on a custom robotic collision avoidance task using ManiSkill3, four robotic dexterous manipulation tasks from the Adroit benchmark, and three image generation tasks, namely inpainting, super-resolution, and deblurring, using the ImageNet-128 and CelebA-HQ-256 datasets. Our findings show that our framework improves sample quality and guidance stability across all tasks, without imposing any additional overhead.

Title: D-MOD: Diffusion mode estimation for accelerated MRI

  • Authors: Md Kawsar Ahmed, Mathews Jacob
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40110
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40110
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.40110]] D-MOD: Diffusion mode estimation for accelerated MRI(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40110)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Diffusion models are powerful priors for accelerated MRI reconstruction, but most current methods are posterior samplers, which return a random draw from the posterior. A reproducible and high-quality reconstruction often requires averaging many samples, resulting in a high computational cost. We introduce D-MOD, a fast algorithm that instead provides a posterior mode. It uses a minorize-maximize algorithm on a lower bound of the smoothed log-posterior, which provably increases the objective. Each iteration draws S noisy samples of the current estimate at the current noise level, denoises them with a pretrained diffusion model, and combines their average with the measured k-space through a conjugate-gradient (CG) solve. We anneal the noise levels, as the distance between the bound and its target decays quadratically with noise. On fastMRI brain (T1, T2, FLAIR), a single D-MOD reconstruction exceeds, in PSNR, the 10-sample finite-MMSE estimates of DPS, DAPS and SNORE-P and stays within 0.31 dB of the strongest baselines (DiffPIR, DDS). This approach thus provides a fast deterministic reconstruction algorithm (11-73 times faster than MMSE estimates), which is useful in a clinical setting.

Title: VideoMemBench: How Well Do MLLMs Remember in Video Conversations?

  • Authors: Zhengdong Hu, Chao Wang, Kaituo Feng, Huanjin Yao, Mingfei Lu, Hehe Fan, Yi Yang
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40131
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40131
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.40131]] VideoMemBench: How Well Do MLLMs Remember in Video Conversations?(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40131)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can now process hour-long videos, making extended multi-turn dialogue over a single video a natural mode of use. Sustaining such a dialogue requires more than answering each question in isolation: the model must retrieve and reuse information established earlier in the conversation, both what it has seen and what it has said, an ability we term in-context video memory. Existing video QA benchmarks leave this ability unmeasured: their questions are mutually independent, never reference one another, and each is answerable from the video alone, so nothing ever requires carrying information across turns. We introduce VideoMemBench, in which each sample pairs a single video with a long multi-turn dialogue, whose turns are connected by typed cross-turn dependencies: an Episodic Dependency refers back to a visual episode described in an earlier turn, probing memory of what the model has seen, while an Answer Dependency builds on the model's own earlier answer, probing memory of what the model has said. Every dependency is verified considering two properties: 1) necessity, meaning the question cannot be answered without its earlier turns, and 2) non-leakage, meaning the question does not restate the information from its earlier turns. To enforce these properties, we design a construction pipeline with three LLM roles: a planner, a generator, and a judger, with each dialogue reviewed by human experts. VideoMemBench comprises 326 videos with 2,476 turns, of which 1,387 depend on earlier turns, including multi-parent dependencies and long-range call-back. Among over 20 evaluated open- and closed-source MLLMs, Claude Opus 5 performs the best with an overall score of only 45.7\%, highlighting the challenge of in-context video memory. Our dataset and evaluation are released.
